People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. that the bank and sometimes even my manager are watching for me to mess up, even when people show me it was just a normal paperwork or register error.\n- key: S020\n  name: Hallucinations\n  description: Perception-like experiences that occur without an external stimulus and are as vivid and clear as normal perception.\n  manifestation: When I’m closing the restaurant or sitting alone in the condo, I’ll hear a low voice say my name or whisper that I’m going to lose everything, and it sounds real enough that I turn around to check.\n- key: S023\n  name: Negative_Symptoms\n  description: A state where emotions, motivation, speech, or social interest that should normally be present are diminished or lost.\n  manifestation: I used to joke with regulars and text my brother after shifts, but now I mostly keep my face straight, say only what I have to, and let messages sit because I don’t feel the pull to answer.\n- key: S024\n  name: Depressed_Mood\n  description: A state where a sad, empty, or hopeless mood persists.\n  manifestation: Most days there’s this heavy, empty feeling in my chest, like I’m going through the motions carrying plates and smiling because been all over the place, where some weeks I barely touch staff meal and other nights I come home and eat whatever’s in the kitchen just to feel settled.\n- key: S028\n  name: Psychomotor_Changes\n so slowed down that grabbing menus and walking to the POS takes me longer than it should.\n- key: S031\n  name: Diminished_Thinking\n  description: Diminished ability to think or concentrate, or just entered.\n- key: S032\n  name: Death_Suicide\n  description: Recurrent thoughts of death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide attempt.\n  manifestation: I don’t have a plan to hurt myself, description: depressive_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness (Criterion C)\n  manifestation: For most of this whole period, even when some days were lighter, I’ve been weighed down by that empty, guilty feeling more often than not.\n- key: additional_requirement_3\n  name: Additional Requirement 3\n  description: I mean, I used to enjoy joking around with the regulars at work, and I’d usually text my brother after my shifts. Now I mostly just keep a straight face, say what I have to say, and go home. Even messages… I just let them sit there. I don’t really feel the pull to answer.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in activities that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. I don’t really get anything out of the stuff I used to. Like with regulars at work, I used to joke around and enjoy that a bit. Now I mostly just keep my face straight and say only what I need to say. And after shifts, I used to text my brother, but now I just let messages sit there. I don’t really feel like answering.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of hurting yourself or ending your life?\n\nPatient: I don’t have a plan to hurt myself or anything like that. But… yeah, sometimes when I’m lying awake before an early shift, I think it would be easier if I just didn’t wake up. Like then I wouldn’t have to keep failing at everything.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past year and eight months?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. My eating’s been pretty up and down. Some weeks I barely touch the staff meal, and then other nights I get home and just eat whatever’s in the kitchen, like I’m trying to settle myself down. So my weight has probably shifted some, but it hasn’t felt steady.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasurable or enjoyable?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, maybe little things once in a while, but nothing that really pulls me in. I used to enjoy joking with the regulars at work, or texting my brother after a shift. Now I mostly just keep a straight face, say what I need to say, and let messages sit there because I don’t really feel like answering.\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods of feeling really down before the past year and eight months?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I’ve had rough stretches before, like anyone, but I don’t know if they were the same as this or lasted long enough to call them periods. This last stretch feels more noticeable to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you had more than one period in your life where you felt this down before the past year and eight months?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I can say more than one, no. I’ve had bad times before, but I can’t really separate them into clear periods like that. This current one is the only one that really stands out to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt this down before in your life, prior to the past year and eight months?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I’ve had times where I was stressed or sad, but not like this, and not where it stuck around in the same way. This is the first time it’s felt this clear to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any other periods in your life where you felt as down as you do now, before the past year and eight months?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I’ve felt sad before, but not like this heavy, empty feeling most days. Like I’m just carrying plates and smiling because I’m supposed to, not because I actually feel okay.\n\nDoctor: Have you had more than one period in your life where you felt as down as you do now?\n\nPatient: Not more than one, no. It’s really been this one long stretch. The really depressed and guilty feeling has been around for about a year and a couple months during all of this.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt as down as you do now before the past year and eight months, more than once?\n\nPatient: No, not more than once. Before this past year and eight months, I don’t remember feeling this kind of down. Now it’s most days—just this heavy, empty feeling, like I’m carrying plates and smiling because I have to, not because I actually feel good.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods of feeling significantly better between the times you felt this down?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, there have been days that felt a little lighter, but most of the time through this whole stretch I’ve still had that empty, guilty feeling hanging over me. It’s been there more often than not.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."
